ParlaMint-GR_2019-11-20-S1-commons.u302	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, SYRIZA's proposal, as it was recorded in the Proposed House, but also in the debate to date in the Review House, is based on a specific legal thinking. This is a proposal for a review of articles which have internal cohesion and linkage between them, and I will, as far as possible, try to explain, at least one section, specifically concerning the architecture of the organisation of the political system, which is exactly what internal cohesion is doing so. The guiding thought was and is to create conditions for strengthening the democratically legitimised government towards institutions that do not draw their legitimacy directly from the people, because the modern world treaty is characterised by the emergence of centres of political and economic power that operate independently of the people's will, by using special methods of discipline. The European Central Bank, the International Monetary Fund, the European Commission in recent years have just exercised such disciplinary powers in the country, through the monumental technology and under the constant threat of bankruptcy. It is another matter that there were governments which took up the package and the package of measures proposed and finally imposed by these external coercive institutions and discipline and another by the SYRIZA Government, which, after the six-month negotiation, was certainly led to a political compromise, but was never taken into account politically and, if you like, this package, this package of austerity measures. In fact, we should point out to many colleagues who insist on calling the third memorandum as the third and worst-case Memorandum, that this package had, as a whole, a budget adjustment of around EUR 5.4 billion, when the budgetary adjustment made by the two previous governments, in any case, was at the rate of EUR 65 billion. Now, how is it possible to compare the EUR 5 billion to the EUR 65 billion and to say that the EUR 5 billion is more than EUR 65 billion? It may be related to special mathematics, which are practised in the New Democracy, where - to remember the late Constantine Mitsotakis - zero plus zero probably does 14%. It is precisely this Memorandum technology that offers experience and raw material to lead to a proposal for a Review that makes this experience a practical policy and leads to concrete proposals to amend the Constitution, which take into account the possibility of optimising it. But look at one more thing. These disciplinary functions, which are carried out by specific mechanisms, are not related to the Memorandum of Understanding alone. A corresponding function is performed in the conditions of supposed regularity and international money markets, and they do so through the special role of debt and its service in modern global architecture. What is this new global architecture? The one that determines the interest rate as a mathematical expression of the credit risk and therefore the markets operate in a disciplinary manner, saying that if you do not implement it as a government of alpha, beta, gamma specific fiscal, labour or other policy, this will lead to an increase in interest rate, which will eventually force you to implement what I have decided to be technically correct political measures. From this global discipline no one can escape, whether they are part and member of a supranational organization or not. This global architecture is ruthless, and it is precisely for this reason that in a modern parliamentary democracy the terms of strengthening the government towards the disciplinary coercive functions of this global economic architecture must be found. So the SYRIZA proposal just strengthens the democratically legitimised government through two first-time choices. Firstly, to promote the constructive vote of distrust and, secondly, to disconnect the election of the President of the Republic from the dissolution of the House. Take note, however, and this is an important element: this proposal does not aim without anyone else to strengthen government stability or to see government stability as self-evident for the parliamentary system. Of course, government stability must be weighted as one of the aims which the Constitution must serve and its arrangements with regard to the organisation of the political system, but that is not the only objective that we need to take into account. On the other hand, we must also take into account many other objectives. And it is precisely these other objectives that may be more important than government stability. And that is why, at the same time as we call a constructive vote of distrust and disconnection of the election of the President of the Republic from the dissolution of the House, we add something else: firstly, the simple analogue and, secondly, the popular referendums, which I see, are treated in a way that reminds us of an aristocratic manner of politics, an aristocratic concept, namely, that the referendums pose a risk that the people will not understand, that the people will not be taken away, that the people do not have the common sense, the one they need each time to make the right decision. It is only in democracy that the only indicator of the rightness or inappropriateness of a decision is the will of the people. This does not mean that we should not take into account fundamental, moral and political weightings. But, in the final analysis, to deprive the people of the opportunity to decide, because it might be wrong, this can lead us to the systems of political organization that have nothing to do with democracy in the end. So the SYRIZA proposal is a single proposal, which takes into account precisely the current challenges of parliamentary democracy and with which parliamentary democracy is confronted, and it can and does try to give answers precisely to the stronger democratically elected government. I had to point out all this because there is a interpretation that SYRIZA, the SYRIZA proposal, is in agreement with all the sacrifices to disconnect the election of the President of the Republic from the dissolution of the House. That is not true. SYRIZA does not agree without anyone else with the disconnection of the presidential election from the dissolution of the House. It sees this arrangement as an element of a broader draft of amendments to the Constitution, which has a specific legal thinking. It is not alone, cut off from the rest of the proposal we are tabling. In other words, it cannot be seen as being cut off, for example, from the acceptance of mere analogous, for example, by the acceptance of the amendments relating to the strengthening of popular referendums and the possibility of intervening in the core of the decisions of modern democracy. We therefore need to see this whole proposal as a single and coherent whole. Now, on the substance of the matter currently dominated in the debate and it is no other than the special arrangements governing or going to govern the election of the President of the Republic: the constitutional situation, as it stands today, provides that, if the increased majority required for the election of the President of the Republic in the current Parliament are not achieved, elections will be broken down, two votes will be followed, with the aim of eighty-eight Members, who, if they do not, then and only exceptionally, will be led to the election of the President of the Republic either by a hundred and fifty-one votes or by a relative majority. But there is a crucial element here, which differentiates the constitutional situation today in relation to the proposal of the New Democracy. And that is nothing more than the mediation of the electorate, from the mediation of the elections. So today's constitution provides for the possibility of electing the President with a hundred and fifty-one votes, but that is what the political system has been through since the suffering of the democratic electoral process. We have here, in other words, a regime which provides incentives to political parties if we want to see it from the positive angle. If, however, we want to see it from the negative angle, it is a threat to the elections, that if they do not consent, we will be led to the dissolution of the House. And this is a threat, obviously, to the government in question, which should have an incentive to go to consensual solutions with the opposition. So we have here a mobilisation from the point of view of constitutional regulation to the political forces, with a positive or negative sign, in order to consent to the President of the Republic, that is to say, to consent to the person which regulates that the rules of political game, at least as reflected in the constitutional text, will be respected. Two comments: Why is consensus necessary or, at least, why is it necessary for the Constitution to provide incentives for consensus? I said that just before. The President of the Republic, in addition to these splendid expressions, is the symbol of the unity of the nation and various other extremely interesting interests which have been heard, are, in fact, in a system such as parliamentary democracy - which is primarily defined and determined by the Treaty of political competition - the minimum degree of equilibrium between the political forces. It is wrong to say that the President of the Republic, allegedly, after the 1986 Review, has been to such an extent far removed from his powers so that he does not play any role. I think it's absolutely wrong. On the contrary, the President of the Republic still has serious regulatory powers, which guarantee that precisely the rules of the game under the conditions of the transition of the political system from one phase to another, namely at the time of the elections and from the time of the elections, will be respected. This means that he must enjoy at least minimum confidence on the part of the political parties, so that no political or other types of contempt are created in his motives, in his ideological purposes, and that he must be a person enabling the political forces to at least have the elementary confidence that he will play the role of regulator at the moment of the transition. All of this is extremely critical of the way in which we understand the role of the President of the Republic, but also of what we are proposing. One parentheses, second comment: I hear a number of critical comments about the right, correct, choice of SYRIZA in 2015 not to consent to the election of the President of the Republic and to lead the country to elections. I have two points to make here. The Constitution, as I said before, gives incentives for consensus. It creates conditions to seek consensus from political parties. Whether or not it is achieved is ultimately a matter of the political assessment of each party. If, therefore, a political force chooses, on the basis of the present constitutional situation, not to consent, then it will be judged. And will be judged by the final judge of all of us, the people. That is to say, the central rule of democracy, which is no more than the political cost. And what happened in 2015, when the SYRIZA Three-SYRIZA chose not to consent to the choice of a person for the Presidency of the Republic? Parliament was dissolved, we were led to elections, and this option was welcomed by the electorate, highlighting SYRIZA as the government of the country. "Greatly right," the Greek people told us, "you did not allow the catastrophic Samara government to continue its work." I am now returning to our proposal, which, I say, is unified. I have heard one more comment, that the New Democracy does not consent to our proposal, which provides for the immediate election of the President of the Republic by the people, exceptionally alone - and I will explain immediately after what I mean - because, allegedly, New Democracy respects the separation of powers. In other words, you want to tell us that in 2014 - and the Special Rapporteur of the New Democracy said so in his place, you can look at it in the Minutes - when the New Democracy had tabled its proposal for a direct election of the President of the Republic by the people, it did not respect the separation of powers and today, in 2019, it respects them. You see, then, that this type of business acrobatics do not help the debate go a step further. So what does SYRIZA's proposal say? It says that, by disconnecting the election of the President of the Republic from the dissolution of the Parliament, we need to find those incentives that create "comfort" conditions or mobilisation of the political forces in order to consent to a minimum of equilibrium. How does this proposal do it? It says that since the first three votes have not achieved the increased majorities, from there on, for six months in a row, the political system will be subject to investigation if there are conditions and conditions to agree on a person who has at least 3/5 acceptance of the House. This means that no political force, since there is no incentive, if you like, of the dissolution of the House to bring about a government change, will enter the process of not consenting to a person who is the least equilibrium, because he will have nothing to gain from it. It is therefore only as a threat that the possibility of a direct election by the people of the President of the Republic, who operates as an exceptional, last resort, may lead to a direct election. So here I come to reply to a legal, technical question which has been raised, I think: how is the SYRIZA proposal compatible with the fact that Article 30 says that the President of the Republic is elected by Parliament? The answer is very simple. SYRIZA's proposal provides for the election of the President of the Republic by Parliament as a general rule and only exceptionally - which means a specific case in relation to the general rule - it provides for direct election by the people. So here we have the very legal construction of the general to specialists, the rule with the exception. Article 30 provides for the general rule, which is followed only as an exceptional solution, which proposes the direct election of the President of the Republic by the people, in order precisely to ensure that such a prospect operates as an incentive for the consent of the political parties. Therefore, a legal, technical, interpretative issue in relation to the consistency of constitutional provisions is not raised. What is being proposed is the political substance. And what is the political substance? The political substance - to close, because I have exceeded my time by four minutes - is that the New Democracy wants to do this. As in a series of other institutions, for about four and a half months now, its political objective is to control public life. Whether we're talking about the state at last, whether we're talking about the CEC or about individual, if you want, institutions and functions in public life, New Democracy seeks their suffocating control. I could name a whole list of such choices that have been made in the last five months. I'll suffice to three, four. See what you've done with S.H.I.E.L.D. See what you have done in all the ECJs. What you chose was to put in charge of ERT the Director of the Press Office of Mr. Mitsotakis, when Mr. Mitsotakis was Chief of the opposition officer. And you blamed SYRIZA for allegedly attempting to control ERT, that is, things that do not in any way stand and through any attempt to distort reality.

ParlaMint-GR_2019-11-20-S1-commons.u365	Mr. Cappato, you have the floor.
ParlaMint-GR_2019-11-20-S1-commons.u366	Thank you, Mr President. Mr Ministers, ladies and gentlemen, in addition to our different approaches recorded in the committee, it is an honour that we are members of a Review House, one of the four of the post-mopulation so far. I congratulate colleagues on all the fact that they have worked in the Commission to reach convergences on revised provisions. Ladies and gentlemen, it had become clear - since the previous parliamentary term of the proposed Parliament and, of course, before the July elections - that for the New Democracy the constitutional review was not as ambitious as it should be. Our view is not aimed at reducing the value of individual provisions that are evident in the end to be revised. Most Members have referred to Article 16. I will not dwell so much on the economic nature of the debate on the need to establish non-state universities. We need to explain to the citizens what the importance of the early update of the articles of the Constitution is. In order to make this easier to understand, Article 16 has indeed acquired a teaching dimension now. Ladies and gentlemen, what do we mean when we say that our country should be turned into an international training centre for higher education and research? Most of the Vice-Population colleagues focus their disagreement exclusively on the possibility of involvement - within the introductory market - in higher education, with which they are ideologically opposed. However, they face the debate in a way of monolithic, which ignores the wider consequences of a revision of Article 16. The New Democracy argues in favour of the need to transform the country into an international educational centre, because this shows a key competitive advantage. It is a development that can be a multiplier for the country. In previous decades, power indicators such as population, economy and military power would be catalytic for the country's international position. Let's be honest. The prospects for Greece on the basis of the classical indicators of international strength would be rather pessimistic in the long term. However, in today's world, the importance of indicators of so-called'soft power' has increased, a term that has been dynamically introduced into international relations over the last thirty years. Mild power is about culture, education, respect for international law, diplomacy, quality of life, as well as many other factors, in general, the influence and attractiveness of a country on the international scene. Based on the theory of soft power, Greece could be a quite important country at global level, in individual sectors. Ladies and gentlemen, what is the timeless basic comparative advantage of Hellenism? But, of course, culture, science and education. That is why the rest of Greece knows mainly about the basis for the worthy system of the Western world by Greek philosophy and thought. When we argue about the need to transform Greece into an international high-level educational centre for students, researchers, academics and scientists from all over the world, we also have this in mind. The leadership of Greece in any area is transformed into a factor of power, let alone when we talk about education, which over time shapes culture, science and economy, ultimately the very concept of progress worldwide and over time. The review of two key articles that we are debating today deserves to highlight the importance of amending them for the stability and quality of our political system. In any case, the central position here today is the revision of the fourth paragraph of Rule 32, which must lead to the disconnection of the procedure for the election of the President of the Republic from possible dissolution of the House. Let us make it clear, ladies and gentlemen, that this is the major one. The revision of the article in question is a streamlining interference in the political system. Unfortunately, the way in which the procedure for the election of the President of the Republic has been used was a factor of destabilisation of the political system and it has certainly been depreciating for the institution, which he has actually 'worked'. Extortion of the dissolution of the House has been abusively carried out by a micropolitical vehicle. Officerial Vice-Presidents focus on the institution's dimension as a balancing element for the city, and that is why they argue in favour of its direct emergence by the people. However, political practice and recent historical experience show the opposite. If we look for the substance of the institution of the President of the Republic today, I think we all agree that it consists of two basic functions. It is a symbol of unity of the nation and a factor in strengthening the normality of political life. A direct election process, which always receives conflicting features, would limit the two most essential to the civilisation of the institution. Furthermore, the election of a president with fewer parliamentary majority voting as a last resort is not the solution to the revision of Article 32. The proposed provision is a method of dealing with a deadlock in the least painful for political stability. It shall be left to the maturity of the political system to achieve convergences for the election of the President of the Republic. Otherwise, we are self-determination and discrediting our role as parliamentarians, when we raise the process of electing the President of the Republic in a field of party controversy, in a process whose success depends on the wording of a text, ignoring an essential condition of the healthy functioning of parliamentarianism, which is the genuine effort to achieve convergence. This latter is not something that can be imposed by the letter of the Constitution. In order to achieve this, it is necessary to reflect the institution of the Presidency of the Republic. Every democracy needs institutional counterweight. Despite its limitations, the Constitution does not prohibit the functioning of the institution of the Presidency as a counterweight - in a certain context, obviously - and if historically necessary in exceptional cases. This is not only a legal interpretation of the Constitution, but also an institutional conquest within the framework of a political tradition. In other words, it is a acquis which will be served by the spirit of the Constitution. A second point we are debating today concerns the revision of paragraph 2 of Article 68, with which the opposition may, on its own initiative, require a committee of inquiry. This is a provision that will enrich our culture qualitatively and constitutes an important concession of the parliamentary majority. It links the control of minority control to the parliamentary mechanism of the Committee of Inquiry, which, to date, is the prerogative of the majority. In addition, the committees of inquiry on the initiative of the Opposition show the pluralist nature of our parliamentary democracy, especially at a time when the debate on free republics in Europe is intense. Therefore, this provision can contribute to the qualitative upgrading of the operation of the House as an additional institutional counterweight to the effective control of the government by the opposition, provided that this parliamentary tool is used by the opposition itself with institutional responsibility. Before closing my speech and on the occasion of tomorrow's debate, I would like to express this hope that this constitutional review will be the beginning of a process that will allow Greek citizens living or abroad to exercise their constitutionally guaranteed right without hindrance. As a member of the Kefalonia and Ithaca Prefecture, I have been representing a place where my compatriots have for centuries been unholying towards every part of the earth and for various periods of time and I know well what it is important that they can decide where they so much love and where they dream of returning. Ladies and gentlemen, Greece was once in the world for the progressive nature of its constitution. A few decades after the establishment of the Greek state we had a constitution that was considered a model and a pioneer. In this spirit we must also address today the updating of the country's statute. The historical dimension of a Review House and the recording of the placement of each and every one of these Steps are part of the institutional tradition and memory of the civil society, an institutional memory that will be the guide to the next Review of the Constitution. We must therefore accept, by accepting the restrictions imposed by the historic juncture, treat the Constitution as a statute law which must be updated in good time in a world that is changing more and more quickly, of course, to recognise our institutional responsibility as members of this House with the work of some of the proposed Parliament on the revised provisions, but also to assume our historical responsibility as co-formulators of the institutional memory of our Republic. Thank you.

That is the proposal I make to the House. This simple and self-evident addition, in my opinion, will allow us to establish a procedure in the Rules of Procedure of the House on the basis of which any amendment to existing law will find its place directly in the law that amends it and not in any irrelevant bill. The only thing you want to introduce from Europe is freedom of establishment for private universities. Incidentally, even if this freedom existed, but it was in force in the regulatory framework of Western European countries rather than Turkey, it could not operate in our country a private university. Thank you.

The New Democracy has never used the President of the Republic as a tool to seize power since 1975. You did it. It is unacceptable, ladies of the SYRIZA, to say that what we are proposing in the review procedure is that the President of the Republic can be elected even by the minimal parliamentary majority, thereby depriving him of the opportunity to act as a regulator of the country above the party disputes. The New Democracy's proposal for the revision of Rule 32 is clear: we have three original votes, the first and the second with two two Members and the third with eighty. If the third vote is not elected President, then after five days, the President will be elected by a hundred and fifty-one votes, i.e. an absolute majority of the total number of Members. And I conclude by saying that if this majority is not achieved, after five days the vote shall be repeated between the two majority and deemed elected President of the Republic which gathered the majority. Finally, ladies and gentlemen of SYRIZA, the Greek people see us - that this government has a fresh people's mandate. We're not at the finish as you were in 2019. We're at the beginning. A new beginning for the Constitution, for a balanced constitution. A new start, however, for our Greece. Thank you very much.
